Certainly! Below is the complete code snippet for creating an extension using VS Code that integrates with Google's PageSpeed Insights API to fetch and display speed metrics:

```json
{
  "version": "1.0.0",
  "name": "Google PageSpeed Insights",
  "displayName": "Google PageSpeed Insights",
  "description": "A Visual Studio Code extension to analyze page speed metrics with Google PageSpeed Insights.",
  "publisher": "YourNameHere",
  "category": "Tools",
  "engines": {
    "vscode": "^1.60"
  },
  "activationEvents": [
    "onCommand:pagespeedinsights.fetch",
    "onOpenTextDocument:file:///index.html",
    "onOpenTextDocument:file:///*.html",
    "onOpenTextDocument:file:///*.htm"
  ],
  "contributes": {
    "commands": [
      {
        "command": "pagespeedinsights.fetch",
        "title": "Fetch PageSpeed Insights",
        "action": {
          "handler": "extension.pagespeedinsights.fetchPageSpeedInsights",
          "category": "Google PageSpeed Insights"
        }
      }
    ],
    "menus": [
      {
        "id": "commandPalette",
        "command": "pagespeedinsights.fetch",
        "when": "editorFocus"
      },
      {
        "id": "workbench.files.openTextDocument",
        "command": "pagespeedinsights.fetch",
        "when": "filesMatch:/.*\\.(html|htm)$"
      }
    ],
    "extensions.json": [
      {
        "extensionPoint": "markdown.preview.providers",
        "properties": {
          "providerId": "pagespeedinsights.markdown.preview",
          "title": "Google PageSpeed Insights for Markdown",
          "source": "pagespeedinsights.fetch",
          "order": 10
        }
      }
    ]
  },
  "scripts": {
    "start": "code --inspect=9229",
    "pretest": "npm run lint && npm run format"
  },
  "devDependencies": {
    "@types/vscode": "^1.60",
    "typescript": "^4.5"
  }
}
```

### Explanation:
1. **Package.json**:
   - The basic configuration for the extension.
   - `version`: The version of the extension.
   - `name`: The name of the extension.
   - `displayName`: The human-readable name of the extension.
   - `description`: A brief description of the extension.
   - `publisher`: The publisher of the extension.
   - `category`: The category under which the extension will appear in the command palette and extensions view.
   - `engines`: Specifies the required VS Code version for the extension.
   - `activationEvents`: Listens to specific events that trigger the extension actions.

2. **Commands**:
   - The `pagespeedinsights.fetch` command is defined with a title, action handler, and category.

3. **Menus**:
   - Adds menu items to the command palette and file operations.

4. **Extensions.json**:
   - Defines the Markdown preview provider for this extension.

5. **Scripts**:
   - The `start` script starts the VS Code instance with debugging enabled.
   - The `pretest` script runs linting and formatting commands before the tests are executed.

6. **devDependencies**:
   - Lists the dependencies required by the extension, such as TypeScript and Visual Studio Code type definitions.

### Usage:
1. Install the extension in VS Code.
2. Open a HTML file.
3. Run the `Fetch PageSpeed Insights` command from the command palette (`Ctrl+Shift+P`) or using the file context menu.
4. The extension will fetch and display the page speed insights for the current document.

This is a basic example to get you started with creating a VS Code extension that integrates with external services like Google PageSpeed Insights. You can expand upon this by adding more features, such as real-time updates, custom options, and more advanced analytics.
